### T19 SQL writer initialization repair — 2026-10-11

**Task status:** T19 remains IN_PROGRESS. Open-loop r1 and r2 produced no accepted scenario report. r2 exited 1 during the first SQL writer setup before measurement, with `SQLException: Can't call commit when autocommit=true` at `OpenLoopLoadRunner.createTemporaryPlayersTable`; the MySQL Connector/J connection defaults to auto-commit. Its log is `build/reports/foundation/T19-openloop-20261011-r2.log` (SHA-256 `39E33DFBAFA9B4408EFC0765708A1252BECBCD1C88C7FA3272602A9E33185406`).

**Discovery and decision:** The runner created a temporary `players` table and committed before it disabled auto-commit. Keep the project-supported MySQL Connector/J connection to the isolated MariaDB endpoint and the existing dialect SQL. Initialize the SQL writer in one explicit transaction: set auto-commit false, create the temporary table, commit, then process normal write batches. This adds no dependency and changes no schema, wire, product API, or benchmark profile. A failure during setup still closes the connection through the existing failure path.

**Regression evidence:** Added `OpenLoopLoadRunnerTest.sqlWriterInitializationDisablesAutoCommitBeforeCreatingAndCommittingTemporaryTable`, using a JDBC proxy that starts with auto-commit true and throws on commit while that state remains true. The test failed before the ordering repair (expected RED; command exit 1; log `build/reports/foundation/T19-sql-init-proxy-red-20261011.log`, SHA-256 `FE778EC1299754BB6067DABA0E1712B3A5F13765143C44AA03D7CCBF5DDCBE83`) and passed after the repair (GREEN; exit 0; log `build/reports/foundation/T19-sql-init-proxy-green-20261011.log`, SHA-256 `1801D99E9C479808D03CFECDB0961D5584F781A13D2BEEC036BE365AF1D17184`). The test asserts auto-commit is disabled before table creation and commit.

**Implementation/review:** Commit `afa82d1f88f2cb88cee66f8c321b2b93a1d4b015` contains only the runner fix and regression test. Independent read-only adversarial JDBC review found no actionable findings; the reviewer did not run tests or contact backends. The existing SQLite/MySQL/PostgreSQL table DDL remains byte-for-byte unchanged. Cross-dialect live integration after the transaction-order change remains NOT RUN; the MySQL/MariaDB matrix below is the required next runtime check.

Calibration input is accepted r4 (receipt SHA-256 `774F1499702D4C59C530674D01BAE4CD167714804A6CCD3B8ABC1EB6ECE03D16`). The checked-in `syncmoney-benchmarks/profiles/redis-sql-open-loop.json` is unchanged at SHA-256 `ff6d5262535962ca54e0038dfed4ab898b25a750ce073f54de48cdf4fee3a33a`; all 60 (arrival fraction, batch size, maximum wait) tuples ran with 120-second warmup and 300-second measurement.

**Confirmed schedule behavior:** In `OpenLoopLoadRunner.main`, warmup is inside the nested arrival-fraction, batch-size, and max-wait loops. The unchanged profile therefore runs 120 seconds of warmup plus 300 seconds of measurement for each of the 60 tuples (roughly seven minutes per report). The profile key is named `warmupSecondsPerRate`, but the executable behavior and T19 acceptance text are per scenario; preserve this run as configured.

**Preflight / credential handling:** No competing measurement process was active. Immediately before launch Redis DB 0 size was 0 and a root read-only SQL query returned 0 rows in `syncmoney_t19.players`; the dedicated `syncmoney_bench@127.0.0.1` credential was rotated to a per-run random value and authenticated by a schema-scoped read-only query. That value was passed only through the hidden load-run process environment and cleared from the launcher process after spawn. It is absent from command arguments, launcher script, logs, reports and this record. A first wrapper attempt used an unquoted relative `cmd.exe` script path; `cmd.exe` exited before invoking Gradle and before creating the log/exit file. The path was corrected to a quoted absolute path. The actual r3 Gradle process is hidden, wrote its initial Gradle daemon-startup line to `build/reports/foundation/T19-openloop-20261011-r3.log`, and has not yet produced an exit receipt. This wrapper issue did not start a measurement and did not create a scenario report.

**Evidence files / acceptance:** Launcher `build/reports/foundation/T19-openloop-20261011-r3.cmd`; log `build/reports/foundation/T19-openloop-20261011-r3.log`; exit receipt `build/reports/foundation/T19-openloop-20261011-r3.exit`. Reports 001–014 passed their row gates. The fourteen-row partial validation summary is `build/reports/foundation/T19-openloop-r3-validation.json`, SHA-256 `fb59798a7e15418901e1b86c3cb332aa2ce19744ca4a4bd2cd53fe09586ce344`; it includes each report hash, gate result and tuple. Latest row 014 report SHA-256 is `e2501e0ab4524040dc4e3ba79b7f2fb2b8cb937207130266e72f6f002f5922f8`. Rows 013–014 (fraction 0.5; batch 1; waits 1/5 ms) each recorded 334,910 offered=accepted=persisted and rejected=0. Still pending: Gradle exit 0, 46 additional unique reports sharing the same invocation ID, all measured response/receipt/durable histograms at least 100,000 samples, all scenario correctness/drain/cleanup gates, and independent empty Redis/SQL postchecks. Do not treat fourteen rows as a full matrix result; T19 remains IN_PROGRESS.

**Active-run checkpoint:** Before the first report appeared, the `OpenLoopLoadRunner` Java process remained alive with no exit receipt. A live Redis DB 0 check returned 20,000 keys while a scenario was active, consistent with 10,000 generated balance/version key pairs; the SQL `syncmoney_t19.players` table remained at 0 rows because the runner uses a connection-local temporary `players` table. These were in-run observations, not cleanup results. Post-run acceptance still requires the database to be independently empty.

Source review confirms `valid=true` is emitted only after workers and batch writer drain, per-account balance/version parity is checked in Redis and SQL, and the run-specific Redis keys are deleted. ### T19 row 037 validator false-positive correction (2026-10-11)

The first `Verify-T19-OpenLoop.ps1 -ExpectedCount 37` attempt stopped at row 037 with `counterConservation`. Report `redis-sql-open-loop-b348c203-a91b-44a0-ad93-ff560a123e4f-037.json` has SHA-256 `f1ed6c201598eb400a6ec1d5603c59a1c9a82572d4763b6f7343ed8149d1193d`, tuple `1|1|1`, and counters `offered=669820`, `accepted=601161`, `rejected=68659`, `persisted=601161`, `authorityRejected=0`. Thus offered equals accepted plus admission rejections, and accepted equals persisted. The checked-in runner source documents that `rejected` increments when the bounded load generator cannot acquire an in-flight permit (`maxInFlight=4096`) or cannot submit to its worker queue; `authorityRejected` is a separate Redis explicit-rejection counter after admission. The report records `queueMax=4096`, `valid=true`, `failureCategory=NONE`, and zero ambiguous, quarantine, money-invariant, and version-conflict counts. Its 669,820 response samples include local admission rejections; receipt and durable sample counts equal 601,161 accepted/persisted operations.

**Discovery / ruling:** local capacity rejection is an expected measured load-test outcome and is not a Redis authority rejection or money-correctness failure. The validator's equality `$rejected -eq $report.authorityRejected` was false. The ignored local validator was corrected to check the admission conservation equations and to require zero `authorityRejected` in the correctness gate, preserving the report's rejection-rate evidence. After the repair, `Verify-T19-OpenLoop.ps1 -ExpectedCount 37` passed with 37 unique tuples, the shared invocation ID `d2978cf2-9a34-4cc5-8725-57f6ceeccd58`, and summary SHA-256 `86a9fa391a0a3f15a45cd61508b41aba86b1a3613a816af88114a85423098832` (exit 0). This only clears the validator false positive; it does not establish matrix completion or a performance disposition.

The reports were independently checked against the exact server and plugin pins. Each contains 11 hash-verified backup artifacts, six backend snapshots, six normal-shutdown observations, and four rollback journal checks; SQL/Redis balance and version parity held at every snapshot. Baseline values were `1000.00/v1` and `520.00/v1`; candidate/rollback values were `1337.25/v2` and `765.50/v2`. Every run recorded six verified `Syncmoney disabled.` markers/process exits, zero failure markers, zero forced node stops, zero pending recovery at all gates, and four verified backend shutdowns. The candidate nodeA journal validated six frames (four RECORD, two ACK) with zero pending; nodeB's journal remained absent through rollback. All six reports shared one source manifest. An offline verifier matched 19 current harness-source hashes in every report (114/114) and confirmed the same two report-time source pins across all six; these two files received only post-report edits described below.

**Report-field correction:** The six captured reports' `quiescence.scenario` prose says “Only two synthetic Paper nodes” even in the four Folia/Canvas reports. Their `invocation.project`, `platform` fields, server hashes, and all six lifecycle `serverRuntime` observations per report identify the requested Folia/Canvas builds exactly; the stale prose does not match those observed identities. The current runner now interpolates the requested `$Project` in that sentence, and the contract test covers the Paper/Folia/Canvas label. This post-report metadata-only correction changes the current runner SHA-256 to `40b9273c6010b5f70ccdea810c53c155d93a68956dc17682aaa6537fb7a65e96`; the contract-test SHA-256 is now `8ac90d4d2b2068d99cf0b841e4c0a9c46c3adde179d43c10b2868f546f4bdce9`. The report files remain unchanged and retain their captured hashes and report-time source pins.

**Scope and limitations:** These are E3 local runtime/isolated-backend observations for two synthetic nodes per run. Both nodes were normally stopped and drain-verified before backup; the harness issued no producer writes between maintenance start and candidate acceptance probes. They do not prove fleet-wide quiescence, live writes during maintenance, Folia/Canvas scheduler safety, licensed CMI/native CMI writer behavior, hard-kill recovery, or general release readiness. A separate Paper `26.3#175` run is supplemental and outside the user-confirmed maximum; it does not extend the compatibility range.

**Interrupted attempt disposition:** The earlier minimum-Paper attempt `paper-1-21-6-48-20261013-055544-1dab6d07c25c49d0b9a41928a5d5c038` stopped during canary and produced no `result.json`; it is not evidence. Its remaining nodeB JVM had the run-specific marker and node path and had already logged `Syncmoney disabled.`; PlugDev had no session record, so cleanup force-stopped only that uniquely identified process and verified exit. The accepted `055917` run above supersedes it. The first `26.3#166` invocation used a nonexistent artifact path and failed before starting services; it is likewise not evidence. The exact official artifact from the T15 manifest was then downloaded into ignored build output, SHA-256 verified, and used for the accepted run.

**Independent report audit and targeted runner re-review (2026-10-13):** A read-only auditor reselected all six exact report hashes and rechecked 66 backup artifacts, 36 SQL/Redis snapshots (including balances, versions, schema and expected account values), 12 accepted Vault receipts, 12 recovery-journal checkpoints, 24 rollback checkpoints, 36 shutdown logs, 24 backend shutdown records, 12 node cleanup records, and isolated Gradle-daemon cleanup. Its corrected audit retained the four Folia/Canvas `quiescence.scenario` labels as a historical report-text defect; `invocation.project`, platform pins, server hashes and six lifecycle runtime identities per report identify the exact requested endpoints. The report files remain byte-for-byte unchanged. A separate focused source re-review found no actionable issue: the per-node rollback journal gate follows each normal stop and is covered by before/outside-loop negative controls; failed shutdown records persist `syncmoneyDisabledMarker=false` before the runner throws; the current runner labels the requested Paper/Folia/Canvas platform.

## T21 player lifecycle scheduler acceptance (2026-10-13)

**Status: PASS for this bounded six-endpoint lifecycle subcase; T21 remains IN_PROGRESS.** The exact user-confirmed platform range was exercised: Paper `1.21.6#48` and `26.3#166`; Folia `1.21.6#6` and `26.2#7`; Canvas `1.21.8#603` and `26.3#972`. Endpoint JAR identities and hashes match the T15 endpoint table above. Each isolated PlugDev run used Java `25.0.4+7-LTS-189`, PlugDev `1.3.0`, Mineflayer `4.39.0`, one `LOCAL_REDIS` server, and two offline clients; Redis database 15 used AOF with `appendfsync always`.

All six selected reports show four successful Vault deposits on the synthetic account (two before each action) and at least two accepted writes still incomplete at both player-scheduler action boundaries. The teleport client and server both confirmed Nether arrival. The logout client either received the exact kick packet or reported `ECONNABORTED` after the command-triggered disconnect; in all cases the server log independently confirmed `T15 accepted-write logout scenario`. Each server and Redis stopped normally, no forced cleanup was used, and the final Redis balance/version was `4.00/v4`.

**Harness discoveries and excluded attempts:** Across 21 run directories, 10 reports failed and one attempt produced no `result.json`; none is counted as acceptance evidence. Earlier predicates incorrectly required a deferred retry marker or Bukkit teleport event, although Paper completed directly and Folia/Canvas could complete with a successful future plus entity-owner confirmation and no teleport event. Some Mineflayer disconnects surfaced as `ECONNABORTED`; they are accepted only with the logout command and independently verified exact server reason. Windows file sharing prevented hashing an incremental AOF while Redis was alive, so the runner now hashes after normal Redis shutdown. Port selection now bind-probes and retries OS-excluded ports. Earlier passing reports are superseded for this six-endpoint selection by the exact latest reports above.

## T21 player lifecycle review repair and evidence correction (2026-10-13)

**Findings and dispositions:** Two independent reviews found that the reusable runner could accept an event-bearing teleport without a successful `teleportAsync` future and that its server logout check matched only a reason prefix without binding the disconnect to `AcceptanceA`. The runner now requires exactly one successful future result and rejects missing, false, duplicate, malformed, or exceptional future evidence. Event-bearing paths still require a non-cancelled successful event sequence; eventless paths require entity-scheduler destination confirmation. Logout verification now binds to `AcceptanceA` and accepts only one matching expected-reason line whose full reason equals `T15 accepted-write logout scenario`; the normal `Disconnected` line from the earlier teleport is ignored. The assertion suite adds negative controls for all these cases and passes 14/14.

The six previously selected real-server reports and logs were preserved byte-for-byte. An offline correction audit applies the repaired predicates to the exact six historical report hashes, verifies all six server-log hashes, finds one successful teleport future and the exact expected `AcceptanceA` kick in each server log, and recomputes sizes and SHA-256 for all 24 stopped Redis data/AOF files. It also pins the original audit unchanged: `final-six-endpoint-audit-20261013.json`, SHA-256 `7f7718c69a8f2e2d37721fb752bad93e47e3a3b551e508f764c2f55d71cd6a37`. Correction audit: `build/reports/foundation/T21-player-scheduler/review-corrected-evidence-audit-20261013.json`, SHA-256 `a68237a9166d17fc94578bab6da38d9e39e4873e19c0a2136b20651d64f3d8cd`. The repaired runner was **NOT RUN** against new real-server sessions in this correction; the existing E2 server observations were independently checked against the stricter E1 predicates, and the old report artifacts remain the evidence source.

**Redis runtime provenance correction:** The original runner reused `$redisVersion` for the synthetic account's Redis version key, so each historical result incorrectly records `runtime.redis="4"`. The paired `redis.version=4` is the account version, not the server binary. Each selected Redis server log independently records version `8.10.1`. The runner now retains `$redisServerVersion` separately from `$accountVersion`; the historical JSON reports were not rewritten. The correction audit records each Redis log hash and the observed binary version.

The review also found that the acceptance probe read the teleport source world after calling `teleportAsync`, allowing a fast Folia/Canvas move to log the destination as the source. The probe now captures the source before the call. This diagnostic-only probe update rebuilt `SyncmoneyAcceptance.jar` to SHA-256 `fb83803013b1ec2a22b7295e42e41dc1c9290e8db0833969cc96324d5e182a64`; candidate plugin SHA-256 remained `55d42c2b72e86fdfd1d36e4f909e140f88256527a8e189c0c699d0cef89935d0`. The rebuilt probe was **NOT RUN** on a live endpoint after this logging-only change. Historical lifecycle acceptance uses the prior acceptance JAR and probe hash documented above; the source field was not an acceptance predicate.

**Validation:** On Oracle JDK `25.0.4+7-LTS-189` / Gradle `9.8.0`, `gradlew.bat test :syncmoney-papi-expansion:test shadowJar :syncmoney-papi-expansion:jar acceptanceJar --rerun-tasks --no-daemon --console=plain` exited 0 (`BUILD SUCCESSFUL`, 53/53 actionable tasks). Log: `build/reports/foundation/T21-player-scheduler/gradle-player-lifecycle-correction-validation-20261013.log`, SHA-256 `ad96eb708269aa644ade24d3c41f9e329512a6954f7717a3f43e48db7c5be474`. The lifecycle assertion suite passed 14/14; all three modified Node modules passed `node --check`; the PowerShell parser, offline correction audit, and `git diff --check` exited 0. Combined focused-validation log: `build/reports/foundation/T21-player-scheduler/runner-correction-validation-20261013.log`, SHA-256 `f643ecba1261590a29f9c9b2b0058faeb94948514a16ef9ae09e127701ab0974`. This closes only the bounded lifecycle harness correction; T21 remains IN_PROGRESS with the broader matrix and licensed CMI gate unchanged.

## T21 lifecycle runner Windows argument-size repair and captured validation (2026-10-13)

**Finding and disposition:** Targeted harness review found that passing the entire server log as a JSON command-line argument could exceed Windows' process command-line limit after ordinary startup or world-generation output. The runner now sends only teleport-future result/failure marker lines and `AcceptanceA` lines containing the expected logout-reason prefix to the assertion process. These filters preserve duplicate, malformed, failed-future, exact-reason, and suffixed-reason evidence; the full-log Node argument is no longer used. The targeted reviewer rechecked the changed filters and found no actionable issue. The six historical endpoint logs and reports remain unchanged. A fresh isolated Canvas `26.3#972` session subsequently exercised the repaired runner and passed; this is current-runner runtime evidence for that endpoint only.

**Validation:** The lifecycle assertion suite passed 15/15; its native Node test output was redirected directly to `build/reports/foundation/T21-player-scheduler/lifecycle-assertions-20261013.log`, SHA-256 `7d891bf4bfc401b239493b806b0aeb19fce85a0ab9097f42a19c0693ac055434`. Fresh `gradlew.bat test :syncmoney-papi-expansion:test shadowJar :syncmoney-papi-expansion:jar acceptanceJar --rerun-tasks --no-daemon --console=plain` exited 0; the directly captured native output is `build/reports/foundation/T21-player-scheduler/gradle-player-lifecycle-commandline-fix-validation-20261013.log`, SHA-256 `1a5e1233115295c8d1bab2716f1e65a5b843c75e28946eb3d8d791ce53c2db57` (`BUILD SUCCESSFUL`, 53 actionable tasks executed). All three lifecycle Node modules passed `node --check`; the PowerShell runner parsed successfully; `git diff --check` passed. Static gate transcript: `build/reports/foundation/T21-player-scheduler/runner-static-validation-20261013.log`, SHA-256 `58694606821e080803f4a24e8925a7f543202a067cdbd0a0b4431624087a80d1`. The direct `javaToolchains` transcript is `build/reports/foundation/T21-player-scheduler/gradle-toolchains-commandline-fix-validation-20261013.log`, SHA-256 `142c39d7f32364fbe2861c2c3f5b4d78caa640f1c8c7d4f33ee141c9af8993b3`; the root build config selects Java toolchain 25, and Oracle/Temurin JDK 25.0.4 are detected. The Gradle 9.8 launcher/daemon itself used `JAVA_HOME` Oracle JDK 21.0.6.

## T21 Folia two-region cross-owner scheduler gate (2026-10-13)

**Historical evidence notice:** The initial reports listed in this section were produced before independent review corrections to the runner, verifier, and client completion predicate. They remain historical records but do not substantiate the final bounded gate. The corrected runs and pins are recorded in the follow-up section below.

**Historical result:** The initial two-player owner gate passed on both user-pinned Folia endpoints. The acceptance probe kept offline clients `AcceptanceA` and `AcceptanceB` connected, moved B to the Overworld at block `(16384, 16384)`, and ran an ownership observation on each player's entity scheduler. Each observation confirmed that the scheduler owned its player's current chunk and did not own the other player's chunk. The two observed positions were over 23,000 blocks apart, in distinct chunks. The initial client completion predicate only observed A's message; this evidence is superseded because it did not prove that B remained active through completion.

## T21 Folia two-region owner gate review correction (2026-10-13)

**Finding dispositions:** Independent review found three acceptance-harness gaps in the historical cross-owner gate. (1) `-CrossOwner` could be passed to Paper or Canvas and then remove the Folia case from `notEstablished`; the runner now rejects non-Folia endpoints immediately after resolving the endpoint pin, before creating a run directory or starting services. (2) The offline proof parser accepted duplicate or suffixed markers and did not reject a valid proof accompanied by `T21_CROSS_OWNER_FAIL`; marker payloads are now end-anchored, repeated tokens and any failure marker reject the report. (3) only `AcceptanceA` received the completion message; each player's entity scheduler now sends a player-specific marker, and the client succeeds only after both clients receive their own marker. The review found no unsafe cross-region entity access. The prior reports remain unchanged and are excluded from final proof.

**Result: PASS for the corrected bounded two-player current-region ownership gate on the minimum and maximum user-pinned Folia builds; T21 remains IN_PROGRESS.** Each fresh run kept both offline clients connected, observed each entity scheduler owning its own current chunk and not the other player's chunk, and received the corresponding targeted completion markers from both clients. Both reports record Redis `4.00/v4`, normal server and Redis exit code 0, and no forced cleanup.

## T21 Paper maximum bounded upgrade report provenance repair (2026-10-13)

**Finding and disposition:** A read-only audit of the earlier Paper `26.3#166` current-candidate report (`paper-26.3-166-20261013-114558-71329cf509c645b89b92b99f80c3afa4/result.json`, SHA-256 `5bdf613bc67a9bde355674df9b488caaffb004878e6a8c82a9fba54fe26ad5c0`) found an Important evidence-linkage gap: the report contained the supplied Paper JAR hash and startup runtime identity but did not record the pinned input path or the generated runtime JAR hash/path. This did not show that the wrong server build was launched. Read-only source and artifact research established PlugDev's two-stage launch: the custom Paperclip input becomes `.plugdev/run/server.jar`, Java receives that path after `-jar`, and Paperclip supplies a distinct versioned runtime JAR. The input/output hashes are expected to differ.

**Repair:** `run-sync-upgrade-cycle.ps1` now records per startup stage the exact input path/size/SHA-256, parsed PlugDev profile source, `.plugdev-server-jar.json` source and prepared source, bootstrap path/hash and symbolic-link target (or pinned-copy classification), bootstrap manifest, and the live run-owned Java process ID plus observed `-jar` argument. It separately hashes and parses the generated versioned runtime JAR and checks its main class, platform title, Minecraft/build identity against the requested endpoint. `platform.serverJarSha256` retains its existing pinned-input meaning; `platform.serverJarInput` makes that path/hash explicit. No application/API, config, schema, SQL/Redis, or recovery contract changed.

**Independent source review:** A read-only reviewer checked the repaired provenance chain and its negative controls and returned no actionable findings.

**Result: PASS_BOUNDED_SUBCASE, E2, current candidate on user-maximum Paper `26.3#166`.** Fresh report: `build/reports/foundation/T21-sync-upgrade/paper-26.3-166-20261013-120651-b9bc43d9b1f5439f873a34f788dc119a/result.json`, SHA-256 `607a155d309b475f5198b21ea5461421e7d22a437f9ae918a6aabb0eef32fbc5`. Java was `25.0.4+7-LTS-189`; isolated PostgreSQL was `18.1`, Redis was `8.10.1` with DB 15, AOF enabled, and `appendfsync always`. Baseline Syncmoney `1.3.2` was pinned to SHA-256 `442a404276b538ce807b0f063fb37920be85e60961a52a475b3f3524d08f4f10` and source commit `71cc55dfeefe9a8f40a3835b051ddd324d353767`; candidate `1.4.0-alpha.1` SHA-256 was `5fdc79aae7ae715f6fcdb3cddbb858ba001855420755c46458102979ca495dbd`.

The Paperclip input `build/reports/foundation/T21-artifacts/paper-26.3-166.jar` was 54,212,025 bytes and SHA-256 `dbaae37e10e30e44ad1ff65cf00992240c3e79d33e150f9c4e833ed0338e8ccd`. At baseline, candidate, and rollback starts, the report links that same path through the generated custom profile, PlugDev marker, `.plugdev/run/server.jar` symlink target and content hash, and the live Java `-jar` argument. The runtime output `.plugdev/run/versions/26.3/paper-26.3.jar` is 30,904,858 bytes and SHA-256 `5399de460a5fe59683202545c1e4c75acc3fdc491cd803af1829b652a7e80231`; its manifest identifies `org.bukkit.craftbukkit.Main`, Paper `26.3-166-cc7b98a`, and build `166`. This output hash is deliberately reported separately from the Paperclip input hash.

**Post-run evidence audit:** `build/reports/foundation/T21-sync-upgrade/paper-26.3-166-20261013-120651-b9bc43d9b1f5439f873a34f788dc119a/provenance-evidence-audit.log`, SHA-256 `dbbb9cc4f87aa3557f8a809da6bfa82b6d0df80d4ac39076ccfd1a3ca6e7343a`, independently recomputed the report and JAR hashes, checked each of the three stage paths against the final profile/PlugDev marker/bootstrap target, and validated the runtime manifest, all eight backup file hashes, four SQL/Redis parity snapshots, the six-frame/zero-pending recovery spool, normal server/Redis exits, and PlugDev CLI pin. All checks matched the report; no run-owned process or listener remained after the cycle.

**Validation:** `pwsh -NoProfile -File tools/plugdev/t21-upgrade-validation.test.ps1` exited 0 with 43/43 cases, including acceptance of distinct valid input/runtime hashes and rejection of an altered input path, altered bootstrap hash, malformed runtime output hash, and wrong runtime build. Retained log SHA-256: `e5c7dc30c1922a23d1369fc4379078c74c1bbaa010cfd6e82d5e510317bf4fd4`. `pwsh -NoProfile -File tools/plugdev/run-sync-upgrade-cleanup.test.ps1` passed 26/26 (log SHA-256 `a851a82f1de6ae7d1a0c449aad984aa50b3face9dd29c6374fbb523322aad196`); `pwsh -NoProfile -File tools/plugdev/recovery-spool-validator.test.ps1` passed 16/16 (log SHA-256 `478b1a7148850b5ee8a0649df070e16008c448c3c73f038d3fa9e03ebef721b7`). Handoff revalidation on 2026-10-13 reran these commands and observed 43/43, 26/26, and 16/16 respectively; the runner parsed without errors and the post-update `git diff --check` passed. The report pins the repaired runner at SHA-256 `f54515431332c3f1e8acf2c530c3a032dcc9224d3ff960fd8be127bfcfb324ba`, validation code at `9a5421f0bcdb407803edce509cc114a45a12140cc8dbd6cdff4159c5cbd43368`, and test file at `4ad47225c520f5d1d462542d5243e51b64ba905535be248def4caf19bdc67904`.

**Shared harness discovery and disposition:** the independent child-cleanup review identified that PowerShell `Start-Process -ArgumentList` joins an argument array into a command line, so a fixture script path without explicit quoting can split when the temporary parent path contains spaces. The regression fixture now forces a space-containing directory and quotes both child `-File` arguments; the cleanup predicate still requires the exact executable and exact accepted argv form before terminating a process. The repaired focused suite passed 121/121, and the independent follow-up review found no actionable findings. Decision: retain fail-closed process identity checks; this is validation-harness hardening only and changes no Syncmoney public contract, configuration, SQL/Redis protocol, or recovery format.

The validator now requires positive integral PostgreSQL process IDs, integral exit codes, and actual Boolean shutdown/start observations. Tests reject fractional and Boolean PID values, fractional exit codes, and numeric truthy flags. `Stop-PostgresBackend` now falls back to a `postmaster.pid` process only after verifying PID, data directory, PostgreSQL start timestamp against process creation time, port, executable, and explicit `-D`/`-p` command-line arguments; a missing listener can use this verified identity for graceful `pg_ctl stop`, while failed identity verification fails closed without invoking stop. The focused tests exercise both no-listener graceful cleanup and rejection when identity verification fails. These repairs change the validation harness only; Syncmoney public API, config schema, Redis wire contract, and recovery format are unchanged.

## T21 M05 PostgreSQL validator hardening and superseding Paper reruns (2026-10-13)

**Discovery and repair:** an independent review found that `Test-T21PostgresProcessIdentity` searched raw Windows command-line text, allowing a `-p` or `-D` substring inside a `-c` value to satisfy process ownership. Reproductions confirmed both embedded-flag forms passed the old helper. The report predicate also used PowerShell coercive comparisons for configuration and process-ownership Boolean observations; a string `"True"` and numeric `1` passed. The validator now parses the process command line with Windows `CommandLineToArgvW`, requires exactly one standalone `-D <expected-data-directory>` and `-p <expected-port>` pair, and rejects missing, duplicate, embedded, malformed, or mismatched options. All observed Boolean evidence in the T21 report/config/process checks now requires an actual Boolean value. Tests retain a positive exact-argv control and reject both embedded-flag forms, string/numeric configuration Booleans, and string/numeric process ownership Booleans.

**RED/GREEN and focused validation:** the configuration Boolean regression failed before the fix because string `"True"` was accepted; after that fix the argv regression failed because an embedded `-p` was accepted. The repaired focused command `pwsh -NoProfile -File tools/plugdev/t21-upgrade-validation.test.ps1` passed 128/128. Retained log `build/reports/foundation/T21-sql-runtime/t21-upgrade-validation-postgres-argv-boolean-final.log`, 8,908 bytes, SHA-256 `a07b09c7b8aae0cfa7cedefede777aa7612def525efd57f35ca74b6b616ff029`.

The report records two accepted Vault deposits, SQL/Redis balance and version parity across normal stop and restart read, cold Vault/VaultUnlocked results of `NaN`/`null`, successful non-forced backend shutdown, and zero cleanup failures. Its offline audit `build/reports/foundation/T21-sql-runtime/postgresql-sync-minimum-paper-20261013-190922-71ff707490f74b8fa636c0057e0b9f1b/offline-audit-final.txt` has SHA-256 `0df5a1ff68521c0897bc71c24a7faaa7731a9590cf2f5c666295dbd7882a7a0c`.

**Superseding maximum-Paper runtime:** fresh report `build/reports/foundation/T21-sql-runtime/postgresql-sync-maximum-paper-20261013-191449-34b6d19652ac484b91eafd6d7143e28a/result.json`, SHA-256 `602a83ddfdb01be4c0fc0f5d3c1e96c2d77de1d53dfd9c966bd76c86e5561b0b`, passed as `PASS_BOUNDED_SUBCASE` (E2) on Paper `26.3#166` / Java `25.0.4+7-LTS-189`, PostgreSQL `18.1`, and isolated Redis `8.10.1` DB 15. ## T21 M05 PostgreSQL argv operand review follow-up and superseding Paper reruns (2026-10-13)

**Review discoveries and repair:** after the preceding PostgreSQL validator hardening, an independent review found the argv scan stopped before the final token, allowing an otherwise-valid command line followed by a dangling duplicate `-D` or `-p`. A second targeted review found that a standalone-looking `-D` or `-p` token consumed as the value for `-c` could still satisfy identity matching. Both were reproduced as failing regressions: the legacy-loop run stopped at the dangling `-D` assertion (`t21-review-finding-red.log`, 3,322 bytes, SHA-256 `7b05b30a5ed23341914002f2b62ed5933ff6aa18e963634caa66945149900cd7`); the pre-operand-skip run stopped at the `-c`/`-D` assertion (`t21-review-option-operand-red.log`, 3,351 bytes, SHA-256 `06765d7412f643f35c4d3bb0423c77dfcdf72fd5d8e417846f49fb590ab292d6`). The parser now scans every argument, requires exactly one exact-case standalone `-D <expected-data-directory>` and `-p <expected-port>`, consumes those operands, and skips the separate values for PostgreSQL 18.1 options `-B`, `-c`, `-C`, `-d`, `-h`, `-k`, `-N`, `-S`, `-f`, `-t`, `-W`, and `-r`. The option list was checked against the installed PostgreSQL 18.1 `postgres.exe --help`. Missing operands fail closed. The validator remains a PlugDev harness change; no Syncmoney API, configuration, SQL schema, Redis wire, or recovery-format contract changed.

**Focused validation and review:** `pwsh -NoProfile -File tools/plugdev/t21-upgrade-validation.test.ps1` passed 132/132 assertions. Retained log `build/reports/foundation/T21-sql-runtime/t21-upgrade-validation-option-operand-final.log`, 9,226 bytes, SHA-256 `223540540da23f875b7301c85643a5be14c859283b683a8b944f690cf8eb7490`. Final validator/test SHA-256 values are `b166c85b60a01a66de72c83b9cee7c8f25ff4b2b2838db727046be24fda080fd` and `29ee66518eb0df68f1c420f730a06ff768fa5fa3208e719a6039c78b85826fc9`. The targeted read-only re-review of the trailing-token and consumed-operand repairs found no remaining findings.

**Superseding minimum-Paper PostgreSQL runtime:** report `build/reports/foundation/T21-sql-runtime/postgresql-sync-minimum-paper-20261013-193517-4d0240e08d1d4f5091c2bb67f6a9c4cf/result.json`, SHA-256 `fa632fa4bad3d923587413dc41bdb4dba77c73b7c8f5e7d6205c7793112eb532`, passed `PASS_BOUNDED_SUBCASE` (E2) on Paper `1.21.6#48` / Java `25.0.4+7-LTS-189`, PostgreSQL `18.1`, and isolated Redis `8.10.1` DB 15. It observed two accepted Vault deposits, SQL/Redis balance and version parity across normal stop and restart read, API classloader PASS, two normal Paper stops, and zero cleanup failures. Offline audit `build/reports/foundation/T21-sql-runtime/postgresql-sync-minimum-paper-20261013-193517-4d0240e08d1d4f5091c2bb67f6a9c4cf/offline-audit-final.txt` SHA-256 `c5169fb625e10c2b7e279aec6d9083e2e96556856c73a4c31441169b58039119` accepted the report predicate; all 39 retained log byte counts/hashes, five artifact pins, and three source pins matched. Post-run checks found zero run-owned processes or backend listeners. The PlugDev checkout was clean at `b59871ae8d9f2731d2bcbca061a735edddd23eb2`.

**Superseding maximum-Paper PostgreSQL runtime:** report `build/reports/foundation/T21-sql-runtime/postgresql-sync-maximum-paper-20261013-194036-7ab918cd903e423e8e057e9731552595/result.json`, SHA-256 `18fee8bf5acea5e9157c534b1b37e6743aa9e5d17c8050794fb789716f2a5e28`, passed the same bounded subcase on Paper `26.3#166` / Java `25.0.4+7-LTS-189`, PostgreSQL `18.1`, and isolated Redis `8.10.1` DB 15. ## T21 M05 current-stable Paper 26.3#178 SYNC runtime cells (2026-10-13)

Current source pins are runner `9da8c0c30dabfee0370446b3cca1c2136110fd4d886ffb50a7e2b9ac2f4adefa`, validator `bf12a605d6f1d172d02bfdbe216274d4709276b4a5aec362f44aca33b2e5bdba`, and tests `c75be5aa3ad72acd8a2d983b4b26b31452b4b457be90ebae1df3d66a920c9baf`. The earlier independent review matched all 12 report/audit hashes, endpoint/backend metadata, source pins, and log counts before the follow-up repair. The audit caught and corrected a transcription error in the Canvas `26.3#972` / MariaDB result hash before the final pass. Current direct report/audit re-audit script `build/reports/foundation/T21-sql-runtime/t21-review-platform-sql-report-reaudit.ps1` exited 0 after all 12 report paths/hashes, adjacent offline-audit hashes, endpoint/backend mappings, schemas/statuses, and current report predicates matched; retained log `build/reports/foundation/T21-sql-runtime/t21-review-platform-sql-report-reaudit-platform-identity.log` is 2,568 bytes, SHA-256 `8fe9ac80a3202aab7831a3d39a5735eadc79b0967c763fa2a46c1ed3fcde5797`.

**Validator findings and repair:** Initial independent review found two false-accept cases. First, the offline predicate compared a report server hash only to the caller-supplied hash, so a caller and report that agreed on the wrong endpoint hash could pass; the predicate now checks the caller hash against the selected endpoint's hard-coded pin before comparing the report. Second, schema 1–3 logs are the historical Paper report format; the predicate now requires schema 4 `platformLogs` for Folia and Canvas while retaining schema 1–3 Paper compatibility. A follow-up review found that schema-4 metadata could still relabel a Folia report as Canvas because the retained stage logs were hashed but their startup identity was not parsed. The predicate now parses both schema-4 stage logs and requires each observed software/version/build to match the selected endpoint; missing, conflicting, or mismatched identities fail closed. The follow-up also found that the wrong-pin regression deleted its fixture logs before checking rejection; cleanup now occurs after that assertion, so the regression exercises the pin guard with otherwise valid retained logs. Regressions cover all four Folia/Canvas endpoint fixtures, a caller/report pair sharing the wrong Folia hash with intact logs, cross-platform relabeling, missing and conflicting log identities, and continued acceptance of legacy Paper schema-3 reports. The relabeling false-accept was reproduced before repair in `t21-review-platform-identity-red.log` (5,859 bytes; SHA-256 `80d23ea3982dd9edab85c629488452663dd3b10cbaa53276d995d4c017b4a79f`); the expected failure was `A schema-4 Canvas report with retained Folia startup logs was accepted.` The repaired focused suite passed 155/155. Targeted independent re-review confirmed schema 1–3 Paper compatibility and found no remaining findings. The validator/test/README changes do not alter Syncmoney runtime, API, config, SQL schema, Redis wire, or recovery-format contracts.

**Setup discovery and remaining scope:** The first MariaDB path resolution used a default `C:\Program Files\MariaDB 12.3\bin` path that is absent on this host. Prior successful local evidence identified `C:\Users\margo\scoop\apps\mariadb\current\bin`; the fresh runs used that local MariaDB `12.3.3` installation and passed. No services or runtime were started by the failed path-resolution attempt. The 12 new results close only the Folia/Canvas M05 `SYNC` SQL backend cells. Other runtime modes/resources, SQL-disabled/local paths, schema migration, fault injection, fleet-wide quiescence, broader scheduler cases, and the rest of T21 remain open or `NOT RUN`; licensed CMI runtime remains `BLOCKED / NOT RUN` because no authorized JAR is available. T21 remains `IN_PROGRESS`.

## T21 M01–M03 supplemental current-stable Paper 26.3#178 quiesced upgrade (2026-10-13)

**Result: PASS_BOUNDED_QUIESCED_UPGRADE (E2).** This refreshes the M01–M03 state/config upgrade subcase on official current-stable Paper after 26.3#175 was superseded. The exact endpoint is supplemental Paper 26.3#178, not a change to the user-confirmed Paper range ending at 26.3#166. PaperMC Fill API v3 artifact SHA-256 is 5dd0c8fb20a96eb7c98f3c4b1c890e99ef6db56fd689a2cf06975a3471fda757. Runner report: build/reports/foundation/T21-quiesced-network-upgrade/paper-26-3-178-20261013-230834-fc0e5210884c453db1dc15311ca1eab8/result.json, SHA-256 64ed0a8ad262b79252651493a9922d6b33d65bacca16d7b9598da34bd502c6df.

The run used Java 25.0.4+7-LTS-189, baseline Syncmoney 1.3.2 (442a404276b538ce807b0f063fb37920be85e60961a52a475b3f3524d08f4f10, source commit 71cc55dfeefe9a8f40a3835b051ddd324d353767), candidate 1.4.0-alpha.1 (5fdc79aae7ae715f6fcdb3cddbb858ba001855420755c46458102979ca495dbd), isolated PostgreSQL 18.1, Redis 8.10.1 DB 15, and local PlugDev checkout commit b59871ae8d9f2731d2bcbca061a735edddd23eb2 (CLI SHA-256 3a600872a3c95bb67b960bdb5efcbb84eb675110f89146566bedb1835ad047d7). The two synthetic nodes were normally stopped and drain-checked before the pre-upgrade backup; the harness issued no writes during the maintenance window.

The 1.3.2 baseline seeded AcceptanceA=1000.00/v1 and AcceptanceB=520.00/v1 through successful Vault deposit receipts. Candidate upgrade-canary-nodeA wrote 1337.25/v2 and 765.50/v2; SQL and Redis matched at each of six snapshots, and both 1.3.2 rollback nodes read and retained those post-upgrade balances and versions. All six startup/version probes matched Paper 26.3#178; both candidate API-consumer checks passed. Schema-version rows remained exactly 1|1 and audit|11 at all six observations. The report records 11 pre-upgrade backup files, each hash rechecked. The candidate recovery journal contained six frames (four RECORD, two ACK), passed checksum validation, had zero pending operations, and remained byte-identical after rollback. No runtime or tests were rerun during that read-only review.

Two earlier complete runtime reports are excluded from PASS: one was rejected by an over-constrained Redis shutdown predicate that treated a clean exit 0 as failure; the next lacked Redis shutdown process-ID evidence. Neither is product-failure evidence. The final report and offline audit supersede both.

This closes only the minimum-Paper MySQL legacy-data read/rollback subcase and the observed additive audit-index repair at schema version 11. ## T21 M04 ADR-15 shared-mode aggregate downgrade gate (2026-10-14)

**Result: PASS_BOUNDED_SUBCASE.** The M04 `LOCAL` SQLite cycle does not create the ADR-15 shared-mode spool by design: `EventConsumerManager` starts `LocalPersistenceWorker` and returns for `LOCAL`; the existing non-`LOCAL`, non-CMI path constructs `RecoverySpool`. The aggregate gate was therefore exercised through the existing M01–M03 SYNC upgrade/rollback runner, without changing M04's LOCAL runner or adding a recovery format.

The six M01–M03 reports listed in the T21 quiesced network-upgrade section cover the user-confirmed endpoints: Paper `1.21.6#48` / `26.3#166`, Folia `1.21.6#6` / `26.2#7`, and Canvas `1.21.8#603` / `26.3#972`. Each ran the existing SYNC path with isolated PostgreSQL `18.1` and Redis `8.10.1` DB 15. Before the 1.3.2 rollback, candidate nodeA's `recovery-v1.jsonl` had six frames (four RECORD, two ACK), passed format/checksum validation, and had zero pending records; nodeB's journal was absent. Four per-node rollback checks per report revalidated the expected journal state, zero pending count, and unchanged hash/absence after normal rollback. Each report retained SQL/Redis parity and the post-upgrade balances `1337.25/v2` and `765.50/v2`. In this turn, all six report files were rehashed against the recorded values and their status, candidate spool state, and four rollback observations were checked again; all matched. Supplemental Paper `26.3#178` remains outside the user-pinned range and is not used to expand this result.

**Fail-closed negative coverage:** `recovery-spool-validator.test.ps1` now includes a valid but unacknowledged RECORD frame (`attempting` payload), confirms it is counted as pending, and verifies `Assert-RecoveryJournalForRollback` rejects it with the pending/ambiguous-work error. No product code or runtime contract changed. Fresh focused validation passed: recovery-spool validator 17/17 (`build/reports/foundation/T21-m04-aggregate-downgrade/recovery-spool-validator.log`, 1,147 bytes, SHA-256 `6043dadf397bafa650f7877e369c36471f5dc90ebad5a51a2027b51bc4b46334`); shared T21 upgrade validator 156/156 (`t21-upgrade-validation.log`, 10,899 bytes, SHA-256 `5fec2e7a2b9dc2873b79c2ce924af3abe87e048ec9573dda691d7e283a9549f0`); and quiesced M01–M03 runner contract 36/36 (`run-quiesced-network-upgrade.log`, 3,010 bytes, SHA-256 `e4dac135eff99221d62e22a6d65d417e431cd3538004d231451a184b7e66339e`).

This closes only the bounded aggregate rollback gate for the exercised SYNC/PostgreSQL+Redis route and the six user-pinned endpoints, alongside the already-passing M04 LOCAL SQLite route. It does not establish pending-work runtime recovery, hard-crash or power-loss recovery, other untested mode/backend/failure combinations, fleet-wide quiescence, or licensed CMI behavior. T21 remains `IN_PROGRESS`; licensed CMI remains `BLOCKED / NOT RUN`.

**Independent review follow-up (2026-10-14):** Review found that the new `pending.jsonl` negative-test fixture was omitted from the test `finally` cleanup. Added it to the cleanup list. A fresh before/after temp-directory check then exposed the existing `absent-at-checkpoint.jsonl` fixture had the same omission; added that fixture too and removed the one uniquely named temp directory left by that verification run after confirming it contained only the zero-byte fixture. Re-ran `pwsh -NoProfile -File tools/plugdev/recovery-spool-validator.test.ps1`: 17/17 PASS, log SHA-256 `6043dadf397bafa650f7877e369c36471f5dc90ebad5a51a2027b51bc4b46334`; no newly created validator temp directory remained. Also re-ran `t21-upgrade-validation.test.ps1` 156/156 (log SHA-256 `5fec2e7a2b9dc2873b79c2ce924af3abe87e048ec9573dda691d7e283a9549f0`) and `run-quiesced-network-upgrade.test.ps1` 36/36 (log SHA-256 `e4dac135eff99221d62e22a6d65d417e431cd3538004d231451a184b7e66339e`). The review finding is resolved; no product runtime behavior changed.

**Independent PostgreSQL catalog review and superseding E2 rerun (2026-10-14):** Review found two validator blind spots in the report above: PostgreSQL expression-index keys have `indkey=0` and were dropped by the catalog `pg_attribute` inner join, while a same-named unique index could appear equivalent to the primary key because the catalog snapshot did not include `indisprimary`. The runner now uses a `LEFT JOIN`, emits `<EXPRESSION>` for non-column keys, and serializes the PostgreSQL primary-constraint flag. The PostgreSQL validator requires the actual primary-key flag and exact eight-field catalog rows; MySQL retains its seven-field metadata predicate. New regressions reject an extra expression index in the lifecycle inventory and a candidate snapshot whose `syncmoney_audit_log_pkey` name is backed only by a unique index. The original review report is in the thread; targeted re-review found no remaining issue with either repair.

**Review finding and RED reproduction:** Independent correctness review identified a P2 evidence-binding gap in the prior minimum-Paper MariaDB legacy-data report: the runner captured the SQL launcher PID but omitted it from final `backendProcesses` evidence, and the report predicate accepted a successful shutdown receipt for a different launcher PID. ## T21 M05 legacy SQL data endpoint-matrix regression closeout (2026-10-14)

The legacy SQL runtime guard now accepts only the three supported products (`MySQL`, `MariaDB`, `PostgreSQL`) paired with the six exact user-pinned Paper/Folia/Canvas endpoint builds, plus the explicitly supplemental Paper `26.3#178` selector. Fresh focused validation passed `pwsh -NoProfile -File tools/plugdev/t21-legacy-sql-upgrade-validation.test.ps1` **46/46**; log `build/reports/foundation/T21-sql-runtime/legacy-target-guard-validation/legacy-sql-validation-final-20261014.txt`, 3,451 bytes, SHA-256 `a4733294ce05178d3f4c02acc078baffd59231c27b6ba564c867d3463a2e5cf4`. The report-specific offline audit script is backend-aware (SHA-256 `811276e12f35863f76611fc13a96386e5927fdd728dc2c70bf088dea624913e7`): it requires MySQL's observed additive primary-only→nine-index repair and requires MariaDB/PostgreSQL's initialized nine-index schemas to remain unchanged, while separately validating each product's schema metadata and report predicate. Each accepted report audit matched the exact endpoint and artifact/source pins, full retained log manifest hashes and byte counts, account balance/version and schema snapshots, normal process exits and backend shutdown, and absence of run-owned process/listener residue. Report and audit hashes for every cell are in the corresponding T21 M05 sections above.

This closes the bounded M05 legacy SQL data compatibility matrix only. It is not a blanket support claim and does not close other T21 modes/resources, SQL schema migration, failure injection, fleet-wide producer quiescence, wider Folia/Canvas lifecycle/owner combinations, or licensed CMI behavior. Licensed CMI remains `BLOCKED / NOT RUN`; T21 remains `IN_PROGRESS`.

**Result: PASS_BOUNDED_SUBCASE (E2), not aggregate M01–M03 acceptance.** The isolated two-node SYNC upgrade and normal downgrade report is `build/reports/foundation/T21-quiesced-network-upgrade/paper-1-21-6-48-20261014-073859-6fa70ab883a845a3bdfb77ca327cafda/result.json` (SHA-256 `66137c93541f19579bed951809a74dce823f74adbc8c4eb548f74e5789ea02f3`). It ran on user-pinned Paper `1.21.6#48` (server JAR SHA-256 `35e2dfa66b3491b9d2f0bb033679fa5aca1e1fdf097e7a06a80ce8afeda5c214`), Java `25.0.4+7-LTS-189`, baseline Syncmoney `1.3.2` (SHA-256 `442a404276b538ce807b0f063fb37920be85e60961a52a475b3f3524d08f4f10`), and candidate `1.4.0-alpha.1` (SHA-256 `8706ed7bfe82f29ff0b6ab4ec316417bb5aa63de0b15cef24e62d735712d5903`). The report pins source commit `faaa94a240cd0759d847119d9278ff34a262ed0c`, PlugDev `1.3.0` commit `b59871ae8d9f2731d2bcbca061a735edddd23eb2`, and quiesced runner SHA-256 `4703b7027a06dd9ba8c4dfec3a21f7603068aee55a74dee8f773fca550eb5537`.

At each of the two candidate shutdowns the runner observed exactly one INFO marker for each required owner. Economy and database reported `drained` with all queue, outstanding/in-flight, retry, stale/drop counters zero, `workerTerminated=true`, and `recoveryBlocked=false`; audit and hybridAudit were explicitly disabled with empty buffer, migration, retry, dead-letter, and drop counters. No owner failure marker or recovery-pending record was present. Baseline and rollback stages completed six normal server stops total; all four isolated PostgreSQL/Redis stop observations were verified, and the report has no runtime or cleanup failure. The candidate recovery journal on node A had six valid frames (four RECORD, two ACK), zero pending records, and the same SHA-256 before and after rollback; node B's journal remained absent.

SQL and Redis snapshots agreed at every observed stage: `1000.00/v1` and `520.00/v1` at baseline; `1337.25/v2` and `765.50/v2` after candidate writes; and those candidate balances/versions remained unchanged through the `1.3.2` rollback reads and stops. The rollback did not restore the pre-upgrade dump or reset versions.

Fresh verification on 2026-10-14: Java 25.0.4 / Gradle 9.8.0 targeted shutdown suite across `ConsumerShutdownTest`, `EconomyEventConsumerRecoveryTest`, `DbWriterConsumerTest`, `AuditLoggerConfirmedPersistenceTest`, and `HybridAuditManagerConfirmedPersistenceTest` passed **33/33** (zero failures/errors/skips); `pwsh -NoProfile -File tools/plugdev/run-quiesced-network-upgrade.test.ps1` passed; `pwsh -NoProfile -File tools/plugdev/t21-upgrade-validation.test.ps1` passed **156/156**; `pwsh -NoProfile -File tools/plugdev/recovery-spool-validator.test.ps1` passed; and `pwsh -NoProfile -File tools/plugdev/run-sync-upgrade-cleanup.test.ps1` passed. The report assertions independently matched the exact endpoint and candidate hash, both candidate owner-drain sets, all six normal server exits, all four backend shutdowns, and zero pending recovery records.

**Limits / disposition:** This is one bounded minimum-Paper runtime observation. It does not establish fleet-wide admission fencing or prove third-party producer quiescence; it does not close the five other user-pinned platform endpoints, the remaining M01/M02/M03 checks, other mode/backend/schema/failure cells, broader Folia/Canvas scheduler cases, or licensed CMI runtime. Licensed CMI remains `BLOCKED / NOT RUN`; T21 remains `IN_PROGRESS`.

For each report, `AcceptanceGuardA` and `AcceptanceGuardB` were separated by moving B 16,384 blocks. Each player's entity-scheduler callback observed ownership of its own player and not its peer. Each independently submitted six synchronous Vault deposits with results `SSSSSF`, received its own rate-limit notice, retained balance `5.00`, and persisted Redis version `5`. The offline predicate joins each canonical UUID across owner proof, transaction outcome, server rejection warning, client notice, and Redis snapshot; it rejects malformed UUIDs and duplicate warnings.

The minimum Canvas server logged both ownership callbacks on the same physical `Region Scheduler Thread #0`; the maximum logged them on different region threads (`#1` and `#0`). The evidence is the per-player `Bukkit.isOwnedByCurrentRegion` result, and the minimum run does not claim separate OS threads. Both reports also passed the existing teleport/logout checks, including successful `teleportAsync`, client/server Nether arrival, exact logout kick reason, accepted writes pending at lifecycle boundaries, normal server/Redis shutdown, and isolated Gradle cleanup.

The reproducible offline audit script `build/reports/foundation/T21-player-scheduler/offline-audit-canvas-guard-pair.ps1` (SHA-256 `a20a5fcd389527c73f301deceaa0923295ac4fcc0ce72e45f63f6ca84feec635`) passed **61 assertions per report**. It reran the proof predicate on the retained server/client log lines and found zero test-port listeners, no run-owned process residue, and no later PID reuse. The two 15-log manifest files have the SHA-256 values in the table.

The initial focused PlugDev red run for the exact duplicate-warning and conditional-report regressions passed **20/23** (three expected failures). After the parser and runner fixes it passed **23/23**. `node --check` passed for both lifecycle JavaScript files, and PowerShell `ParseFile` reported no errors for the lifecycle runner and offline audit script.
